\displaystyle\phantom{=}-619071617270\big]\sqrt[4]{\sin^{3}(\pi x)(\cos(\pi x)+1)}\Bigg\}\,.

(A.46)

For degenerate states at level 3 the expressions are different, but we report here only one for simplicity.

Three-states Potts model

We conclude this appendix with the TSD between the first-level descendant of
\varepsilon
and the primary state itself in the Potts model:

\displaystyle T^{(2)}_{L_{-1}|{\varepsilon}\rangle,|{\varepsilon}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}F\!\left(-\tfrac{8}{5},-\tfrac{1}{5};-\tfrac{2}{5};\eta\right){}^{2}\sin^{4}\!\left(\frac{\pi x}{2}\right)(4\cos(\pi x)+\cos(2\pi x)+19)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{5200}\Bigg\{(F\!\left(-\tfrac{8}{5},-\tfrac{1}{5};-\tfrac{2}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{10}\!\left(\frac{\pi x}{2}\right)\cos^{8}\!\left(\frac{\pi x}{2}\right)\csc^{6}(\pi x)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\bigg[(41600F\!\left(-\tfrac{3}{5},\tfrac{4}{5};\tfrac{3}{5};\eta\right)(2\cos(\pi x)+\cos(2\pi x)-15)+32\cos^{2}\!\left(\frac{\pi x}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\bigg(9\sin(\pi x)\left(49F\!\left(\tfrac{12}{5},\tfrac{19}{5};\tfrac{18}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{3}(\pi x)+260F\!\left(\tfrac{7}{5},\tfrac{14}{5};\tfrac{13}{5};\eta\right)\sin(2\pi x)\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+520F\!\left(\tfrac{2}{5},\tfrac{9}{5};\tfrac{8}{5};\eta\right)(31\cos(2\pi x)+9)\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\left(1782579200\ 2^{2/5}\Gamma\left(-\frac{8}{5}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{17}{10}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{12}{5}\right)\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\Bigg\{\Gamma\left(-\frac{2}{5}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{3}{10}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{13}{5}\right)F\!\left(\tfrac{6}{5},\tfrac{13}{5};\tfrac{12}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{\frac{28}{5}}(\pi x)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\bigg[544F\!\left(\tfrac{6}{5},\tfrac{13}{5};\tfrac{12}{5};\eta\right)(9600\cos(\pi x)-17164\cos(2\pi x)+15129\cos(4\pi x)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-1293)+208\sin^{2}(\pi x)\bigg(9016F\!\left(\tfrac{26}{5},\tfrac{33}{5};\tfrac{32}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{6}(\pi x)+17F\!\left(\tfrac{11}{5},\tfrac{18}{5};\tfrac{17}{5};\eta\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times(3697\cos(\pi x)+6479\cos(3\pi x)+800)+79488F\!\left(\tfrac{21}{5},\tfrac{28}{5};\tfrac{27}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{4}(\pi x)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\cos(\pi x)+99F\!\left(\tfrac{16}{5},\tfrac{23}{5};\tfrac{22}{5};\eta\right)\sin^{2}(\pi x)(1373\cos(2\pi x)+843)\bigg)\bigg]\Bigg\}\,,

(A.47)

where
F\equiv\;_{2}F_{1}
is the hypergeometric function and
\eta=\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\pi x}{2}\right)
.
We computed also TSDs for higher-level descendants, but the expressions are more complicated and we won’t show them here.

Anhang
B
Conventions and further details on the fermionic models

B.1
Conventions on fermions and Dirac matrices

We collect here our conventions on Dirac matrices and Dirac and Weyl fermions.
They are employed in chap.
4
and were also used in
[
BB19aab
,
BB19aac
,
BB20aa
]
.

In a four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime we use a flat metric
\eta_{ab}
.
The Dirac matrices
\gamma^{a}
satisfy

\{\gamma^{a},\gamma^{b}\}=2\eta^{ab}\,,

(B.1)

and the conjugate Dirac spinor
\overline{\psi}
is defined using
\beta=i\gamma^{0}
by

\overline{\psi}=\psi^{\dagger}\beta\;.

(B.2)

The hermitian chiral matrix
\gamma^{5}
is given by

\gamma^{5}=-i\gamma^{0}\gamma^{1}\gamma^{2}\gamma^{3}\,,

(B.3)

and used to define the chiral projectors

P_{L}=\frac{\mathbb{I}+\gamma_{5}}{2}\,,\qquad P_{R}=\frac{\mathbb{I}-\gamma_{5}}{2}\,,

(B.4)

that split a Dirac spinor
\psi
into its left and right Weyl components

\psi=\lambda+\rho\;,\qquad\lambda=P_{L}\psi\;,\quad\rho=P_{R}\psi\;.

(B.5)

The charge conjugation matrix
C
satisfies

C\gamma^{a}C^{-1}=-\gamma^{aT}\;,

(B.6)

it is antisymmetric and used to define the charge conjugation of the spinor
\psi
by

\psi_{c}=C^{-1}\overline{\psi}^{T}\,,

(B.7)

for which the roles of particle and antiparticle get interchanged.
Note that a chiral spinor
\lambda
has its charge conjugated field
\lambda_{c}
of opposite chirality.
A Majorana spinor
\mu
is a spinor that equals its charged conjugated spinor

\mu=\mu_{c}\;.

(B.8)

This constraint is incompatible with the chiral constraint, and Majorana-Weyl spinors do not exist in 4 dimensions.

We find it convenient, as a check on our formulae, to use the chiral representation of the gamma matrices.
In terms of
2\times 2
blocks they are given by

\gamma^{0}=-i\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbb{I}\\
\mathbb{I}&0\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\gamma^{i}=-i\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sigma^{i}\\
-\sigma^{i}&0\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.9)

where
\sigma^{i}
are the Pauli matrices, so that

\gamma^{5}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbb{I}&0\\
0&-\mathbb{I}\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad\beta=i\gamma^{0}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbb{I}\\
\mathbb{I}&0\end{array}\right)\;.

(B.10)

The chiral representation makes evident that the Lorentz generators in the spinor space

M^{ab}=\frac{1}{4}[\gamma^{a},\gamma^{b}]=\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{ab}

take a block diagonal form

M^{0i}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma^{i}&0\\
0&-\sigma^{i}\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad M^{ij}=\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{ijk}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma^{k}&0\\
0&\sigma^{k}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.11)

and do not mix the chiral components of a Dirac spinor (as
\gamma^{5}
is also block diagonal).
The usual two-dimensional Weyl spinors appear inside a four-dimensional Dirac spinor as follows

\psi=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}l\\
r\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad\lambda=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}l\\
0\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad\rho=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
r\end{array}\right)

(B.12)

where
l
and
r
indicate two-dimensional independent spinors of opposite chirality.
In the chiral representation one may take the charge conjugation matrix
C
to be given by

C=\gamma^{2}\beta=-i\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sigma^{2}&0\\
0&-\sigma^{2}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.13)

and satisfies

C=-C^{T}=-C^{-1}=-C^{\dagger}=C^{*}\,,

(B.14)

and we remark that some of these relations are representation dependent.
In the chiral representation the Majorana constraint (
B.8
) takes the form

\mu=\mu_{c}\qquad\to\qquad\left(\begin{array}[]{c}l\\
r\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}i\sigma^{2}r^{*}\\
-i\sigma^{2}l^{*}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.15)

which shows that the two-dimensional spinors
l
and
r
cannot be independent.
The Majorana condition can be solved in terms of the single two-dimensional left-handed spinor
l
as

\mu=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}l\\
-i\sigma^{2}l^{*}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.16)

which, evidently, contains the four-dimensional chiral spinors
\lambda
and
\lambda_{c}
defined by

\lambda=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}l\\
0\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad\lambda_{c}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
-i\sigma^{2}l^{*}\end{array}\right)\;.

(B.17)

In a four-dimensional spinors notation one can write

\mu=\lambda+\lambda_{c}\;.

(B.18)

Alternatively, the Majorana condition can be solved in terms of the two-dimensional right-handed spinor
r
as

\mu=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}i\sigma^{2}r^{*}\\
r\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.19)

which contains the four-dimensional chiral spinors
\rho
and
\rho_{c}

\rho=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
r\end{array}\right)\;,\qquad\rho_{c}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}i\sigma^{2}r^{*}\\
0\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.20)

and
\mu=\rho+\rho_{c}
. This solution is of course the same as the previous one,
as one may identify
\lambda=\rho_{c}
.

The explicit dictionary between Weyl and Majorana spinors shows that the field theory
of a Weyl spinor is equivalent to that of a Majorana spinor, as Lorentz symmetry fixes uniquely their actions, which are bound to be identical.

Finally, we normalize our
\epsilon
symbols by
\epsilon_{0123}=-1
and
\epsilon^{0123}=1
, so that

\frac{1}{4}{\rm tr}(\gamma^{5}\gamma^{a}\gamma^{b}\gamma^{c}\gamma^{d})=i\epsilon^{abcd}\;.

(B.21)

B.2
Metric, vielbein and related connections

In a Lorentzian spacetime we use a metric
g_{\mu\nu}
which is covariantly constant

\nabla_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\lambda}g_{\mu\nu}-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\lambda\mu}g_{\nu\rho}-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\lambda\nu}g_{\mu\rho}=0\,,

(B.22)

so that the Levi-Civita connection
\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}
is given by

\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}g^{\lambda\rho}\left(\partial_{\mu}g_{\nu\rho}+\partial_{\nu}g_{\mu\rho}-\partial_{\rho}g_{\mu\nu}\right)\,.

(B.23)

The covariant derivative on a vector field acts as

\nabla_{\mu}V^{\lambda}=\partial_{\mu}V^{\lambda}+\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\lambda}V^{\nu}\,,

(B.24)

and we remind our conventions for the curvature tensor

[\nabla_{\mu},\nabla_{\nu}]V^{\lambda}=R_{\mu\nu}{}^{\lambda}{}_{\rho}V^{\rho}\,,\quad R_{\mu\nu}=R_{\mu\lambda\nu}{}^{\lambda}\,,\quad R=R^{\mu}{}_{\mu}\,.

(B.25)

Introducing the vielbein
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
by setting

g_{\mu\nu}=e_{\mu}{}^{a}\,e_{\nu}{}^{b}\,\eta_{ab}\,,

(B.26)

we gain a new gauge symmetry, namely the local Lorentz transformations of the tangent space; in this way we are able to couple a fermionic theory to gravity.
Latin letters are used for indices of the tangent space, and
\eta_{ab}
is a Minkowski metric.
In four dimensions, we refer to
e_{\mu}{}^{a}
as the vierbein.
The covariant derivative now needs a corresponding connection, i.e. the spin connection, which in the absence of torsion is defined by requiring the vielbein to be covariantly constant

\nabla_{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{a}=\partial_{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{a}-\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}e_{\lambda}{}^{a}+\omega_{\mu}{}^{a}{}_{b}e_{\nu}{}^{b}=0\,.

(B.27)

This condition can be solved by

\omega_{\mu}{}^{ab}=e^{b\nu}\left(\Gamma^{\lambda}_{\mu\nu}e_{\lambda}{}^{a}-\partial_{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{a}\right)\,,

(B.28)

or equivalently

\displaystyle\omega_{\mu}{}^{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}e^{a\lambda}e^{b\nu}e_{c\mu}\left(\partial_{\nu}e_{\lambda}{}^{c}-\partial_{\lambda}e_{\nu}{}^{c}\right)+\frac{1}{2}e^{a\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{b}-\partial_{\nu}e_{\mu}{}^{b}\right)-\frac{1}{2}e^{b\nu}\left(\partial_{\mu}e_{\nu}{}^{a}-\partial_{\nu}e_{\mu}{}^{a}\right)

(B.29)

in which it is manifest that
\omega_{\mu}{}^{ab}+\omega_{\mu}{}^{ba}=0
.
Then, the covariant derivative acts on a spinor
\psi
as

\nabla_{\mu}\psi=\partial_{\mu}\psi+\frac{1}{2}\omega_{\mu ab}M^{ab}\psi\,,

(B.30)

where
M^{ab}
are the generators of the Lorentz group, which in our conventions are made explicit in eq. (
B.11
).

B.3
The stress tensor of the Bardeen model

In chap.
4
we have defined the stress tensor of the Bardeen model as

T^{\mu}{}_{a}=\frac{1}{e}\frac{\delta S}{\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}}\,,

(B.31)

where
S=\int\,d^{4}x{\cal L}
is the action associated to the lagrangian
(
4.9
).
We review here its classical properties, which in sec.
4.1
we only need in the flat space limit.
The model depends on the background fields
e_{\mu}{}^{a},A_{\mu},B_{\mu}
, and
statisfies various background gauge symmetries, responsible for the properties of the gauge currents
and stress tensor.
Let us discuss the latter.

The infinitesimal background symmetries associated to general coordinate invariance
(with infinitesimal local parameters
\xi^{\mu}
), local Lorentz invariance
(with infinitesimal local parameters
\omega_{ab}
), and Weyl invarance
(with infinitesimal local parameter
\sigma
), take the form

\displaystyle\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}

\displaystyle=\xi^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}e_{\mu}{}^{a}+(\partial_{\mu}\xi^{\nu})e_{\nu}{}^{a}+\omega^{a}{}_{b}e_{\mu}{}^{b}+\sigma e_{\mu}{}^{a}\,,

(B.32)

\displaystyle\delta A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\xi^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+(\partial_{\mu}\xi^{\nu})A_{\nu}\,,

\displaystyle\delta B_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\xi^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}B_{\mu}+(\partial_{\mu}\xi^{\nu})B_{\nu}\,,

\displaystyle\delta\psi

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}\psi+\frac{1}{4}\omega_{ab}\gamma^{ab}\psi-\frac{3}{2}\sigma\psi\,.

Under the Weyl symmetry
\delta_{\sigma}
with local parameter
\sigma
, the gauge fields do not transform,
and the invariance of the action implies

\displaystyle\delta_{\sigma}S

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}x\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}}\delta_{\sigma}e_{\mu}{}^{a}+\frac{\delta_{R}S}{\delta\psi}\delta_{\sigma}\psi+\delta_{\sigma}\overline{\psi}\frac{\delta_{L}S}{\delta\overline{\psi}}\right)

(B.33)

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}xe\,T^{\mu}{}_{a}\delta_{\sigma}e_{\mu}{}^{a}=\int d^{4}xe\,T^{a}{}_{a}\sigma=0\,,

where we have used left and right derivatives for the Grassmann valued fields and in the second line we have implemented the equations of motion of the spinor fields.
Thus local Weyl invariance implies tracelessness of the stress tensor, with the trace computed
with the vierbein as
T^{a}{}_{a}=T^{\mu}{}_{a}e_{\mu}{}^{a}
.
Thus, the stress tensor is traceless at the classical level.

Similarly, the Lorentz symmetry
\delta_{\omega}
with local parameters
\omega_{ab}
implies

\displaystyle\delta_{\omega}S

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}x\left(\frac{\delta S}{\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}}\delta_{\omega}e_{\mu}{}^{a}+\frac{\delta_{R}S}{\delta\psi}\delta_{\omega}\psi+\delta_{\omega}\overline{\psi}\frac{\delta_{L}S}{\delta\overline{\psi}}\right)

(B.34)

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}xe\,T^{\mu}{}_{a}\delta_{\omega}e_{\mu}{}^{a}=\int d^{4}xe\,T^{\mu}{}_{a}\omega^{a}{}_{b}e_{\mu}{}^{b}=\int d^{4}xe\,T^{ba}\omega_{ab}=0\,,

and constrains the antisymmetric part of the stress tensor to vanish on-shell.
Again, we have used the fermionic equations of motion and the fact that the gauge fields

A_{\mu}
and
B_{\mu}
do not transform under local Lorentz transformations. Considering the
arbitrariness of the local parameters and their antisymmetry,
\omega_{ab}=-\omega_{ba}
,
we recognize that the stress tensor with flat indices is symmetric,
T^{ab}=T^{ba}
.
At the quantum level, our PV regularization preserves this symmetry, and no anomalies can arise
in the local Lorentz sector.

Finally, a suitable conservation law of the stress tensor arises as a consequence of the infinitesimal
diffeomorphism invariance
\delta_{\xi}
. It is actually useful to combine it with
additional local Lorentz and gauge symmetries (with composite parameters),
so to obtain a conservation in the following way

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi}S

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}x\biggl(\frac{\delta S}{\delta e_{\mu}{}^{a}}\delta_{\xi}e_{\mu}{}^{a}+\frac{\delta_{R}S}{\delta\psi}\delta_{\xi}\psi+\delta_{\xi}\overline{\psi}\frac{\delta_{L}S}{\delta\overline{\psi}}+\frac{\delta S}{\delta A_{\mu}^{\alpha}}\delta_{\xi}A_{\mu}^{\alpha}+\frac{\delta S}{\delta B_{\mu}^{\alpha}}\delta_{\xi}B_{\mu}^{\alpha}\biggr)

(B.35)

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}xe\,\biggl(T^{\mu}{}_{a}{\cal L}_{\xi}e_{\mu}{}^{a}+J^{\mu\alpha}{\cal L}_{\xi}A_{\mu}^{\alpha}+J_{5}^{\mu\alpha}{\cal L}_{\xi}B_{\mu}^{\alpha}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\int d^{4}xe\,\biggl(T^{\mu}{}_{a}\nabla_{\mu}\xi^{a}+J^{\mu\alpha}\xi^{\nu}\hat{F}_{\nu\mu}^{\alpha}+J_{5}^{\mu\alpha}\xi^{\nu}\hat{G}_{\nu\mu}^{\alpha}\biggr)

\displaystyle=-\int d^{4}xe\,\xi^{a}\biggl(\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu}{}_{a}-J^{b\alpha}\hat{F}_{ab}^{\alpha}-J_{5}^{b\alpha}\hat{G}_{ab}^{\alpha}\biggr)=0\,,

where in the second line we have used the fermion equations of motion
(and denoted with Lie derivatives
{\cal L}_{\xi}
the transformation rules under diffeomorphisms),
and in the third line
added for free to the Lie derivative of the vierbein
a spin connection term (it amounts to a local Lorentz transformation with composite parameter,
and it drops out once the stress tensor is symmetric),
and to the Lie derivatives of the gauge fields suitable gauge transformations
(which also drop out after partial integration as the corresponding currents are covariantly conserved,
recall eqs. (
4.7
) and (
4.8
)
which we use here in their curved space version), and then integrated by parts.
The arbitrariness of the local parameters
\xi^{a}(x)
allows to derive
a covariant conservation law, which contains a contribution from the gauge fields (that would vanish
once the gauge fields are made dynamical, which allows to use their equations of motion).
It reads

\nabla_{\mu}T^{\mu}{}_{a}=J^{b\alpha}\hat{F}_{ab}^{\alpha}+J_{5}^{b\alpha}\hat{G}_{ab}^{\alpha}\;.

(B.36)

Our PV regularization preserves diffeomorphism invariance,
and thus no anomaly may appear in the Ward identities arising from this symmetry.

We end this appendix by presenting the explicit expression of the stress
tensor for the Bardeen model in flat spacetime:

T_{ab}=\frac{1}{4}\overline{\psi}\Big(\gamma_{a}\overset{\leftrightarrow}{D}_{b}(A,B)+\gamma_{b}\overset{\leftrightarrow}{D}_{a}(A,B)\Big)\psi\,,

(B.37)

where
\overset{\leftrightarrow}{D}_{a}(A,B)=D_{a}(A,B)-\overset{\leftarrow}{D}_{a}(A,B)
,
with the second derivative meaning here
\overset{\leftarrow}{D}_{a}(A,B)=\overset{\leftarrow}{\partial}_{a}-A_{a}-B_{a}\gamma^{5}
.
One can verify explicitly all the statements about the classical
background symmetry derived above, namely

\partial_{a}T^{ab}=\overline{\psi}\gamma_{a}(\hat{F}^{ab}+\hat{G}^{ab}\gamma^{5})\psi\;,\qquad T^{ab}=T^{ba}\;,\qquad T^{a}{}_{a}=0\;.

(B.38)

The PV regularization used in the main text
preserves the corresponding quantum Ward identities except the last one,
as the mass term in curved space is not Weyl invariant, and a trace anomaly develops.

B.4
Heat kernel traces

We outline here how to compute the anomalies in the Bardeen model and present some intermediate results.
Similarly, one also proceeds in the case of the MAT background.

In Bardeen model, the role of the operator
H
introduced in sec.
2.3.3
is played by the regulators
R_{\psi}
and
R_{\psi_{c}}
.
Focusing on the regulator
{R}_{\psi}=-D\!\!\!\!/\,^{2}(A,B)
, we expand it as

\displaystyle{R}_{\psi}

\displaystyle=-D^{a}(A)D_{a}(A)+B^{2}-\gamma^{5}\left(D^{a}(A)B_{a}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{ab}\left[\hat{F}_{ab}-4B_{a}B_{b}+\gamma^{5}\left(\hat{G}_{ab}-4B_{a}D_{b}(A)\right)\right]\,,

(B.39)

where
\hat{F}_{ab}
and
\hat{G}_{ab}
are the Bardeen curvatures given in (
4.5
), the covariant derivative is
D_{a}(A)=\partial_{a}+A_{a}
, the covariant divergence of
B_{a}
is
D^{a}(A)B_{a}=(\partial^{a}B_{a})+[A^{a},B_{a}]
and
B^{2}=B^{a}B_{a}
.
We compare this operator to the generic operator
H
in (
2.95
), where in this case we consider it in flat space and with only a gauge covariant derivative with connection
W_{a}
, i.e.
H
takes the form

H=-\Box+V=-\partial^{a}\partial_{a}-2W^{a}\partial_{a}-(\partial_{a}W^{a})-W^{2}+V\,.

(B.40)

Comparing with (
B.4
) we identify

\displaystyle W_{a}

\displaystyle=A_{a}+\gamma_{ab}\gamma^{5}B^{b}\,,

(B.41)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=-2B^{2}-\gamma^{5}\left(D^{a}(A)B_{a}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}\,,

(B.42)

\displaystyle{\cal F}_{ab}

\displaystyle=F_{ab}(A)+\left(\gamma_{ac}\gamma_{bd}-\gamma_{bc}\gamma_{ad}\right)B^{c}B^{d}+\gamma^{5}\left(\gamma_{ca}D_{b}(A)B^{c}-\gamma_{cb}D_{a}(A)B^{c}\right)\,,

(B.43)

with

{\cal F}_{ab}=\partial_{a}W_{b}-\partial_{b}W_{a}+[W_{a},W_{b}]\,.

(B.44)

Now the coefficient
a_{2}(R_{\psi})
, which enters in the anomaly calculations in four dimensions, can be made explicit using (
2.101
) in flat space.
In this limit, which is the one of interest for the Bardeen model, it reduces to

a_{2}(x,H)=\frac{1}{2}V^{2}-\frac{1}{6}\Box V+\frac{1}{12}{\cal F}_{ab}^{2}\,,

(B.45)

and the Dirac traces can be directly computed.
We list here some intermediate results, grouping together the contributions from the same terms in (
B.45
).
We find (with
D_{a}=D_{a}(A)
,
DB=D_{a}B^{a}
,
B^{2}=B^{a}B_{a}
, and so on):

i)
from
a_{2}=\frac{1}{2}V^{2}

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{5}T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\bigg[\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{abcd}\hat{F}_{ab}\hat{F}_{cd}+4\{B^{2},DB\}\bigg]\,,

(B.46)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\left[-\hat{F}^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}+8B^{4}+2DBDB\right]\,,

(B.47)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}\left[-\hat{F}^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}+8B^{4}+2DBDB\right]\,.

(B.48)

ii)
from
a_{2}=-\frac{1}{6}\nabla^{2}V

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{5}T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\bigg[-\frac{2}{3}i\epsilon^{abcd}\left(B_{a}B_{b}\hat{F}_{cd}+2B_{a}\hat{F}_{bc}B_{d}+\hat{F}_{ab}B_{c}B_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{2}{3}D^{2}DB+\frac{1}{3}[\hat{F}^{ab},\hat{G}_{ab}]+\frac{4}{3}[D^{a}\hat{F}_{ab},B^{b}]-2\{B^{2},DB\}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+4B_{a}DBB^{a}\bigg]\,,

(B.49)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\bigg[\frac{i}{6}\epsilon^{abcd}\left([\hat{G}_{ab},\hat{F}_{cd}]-4[B_{a},D_{b}\hat{F}_{cd}]\right)+8\left(B_{a}B^{2}B^{a}-B^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{4}{3}D^{2}B^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\left(\hat{F}^{ab}B_{a}B_{b}+2B_{a}\hat{F}^{ab}B_{b}+B_{a}B_{b}\hat{F}^{ab}\right)\bigg]\,,

(B.50)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}\bigg[\frac{4}{3}D^{2}B^{2}\bigg]\,.

(B.51)

iii)
from
a_{2}=\frac{1}{12}{\cal F}_{ab}^{2}

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{5}T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\bigg[i\epsilon^{abcd}\left(\frac{1}{6}\hat{G}_{ab}\hat{G}_{cd}-\frac{2}{3}\{\hat{F}_{ab},B_{c}B_{d}\}+\frac{16}{3}B_{a}B_{b}B_{c}B_{d}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{8}{3}\{B^{2},DB\}+\frac{4}{3}\{\{B^{a},B^{b}\},D_{a}B_{b}\}\bigg]\,,

(B.52)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[T^{\alpha}a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}T^{\alpha}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\hat{F}^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}-\frac{4}{3}\{\hat{F}_{ab},B^{a}B^{b}\}+\frac{8}{3}\left(B_{a}B_{b}B^{a}B^{b}-B^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-8B_{a}B^{2}B^{a}-\frac{4}{3}D_{a}B_{b}D^{a}B^{b}-\frac{2}{3}DBDB\bigg]\,,

(B.53)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\left[a_{2}\left({R}_{\psi}\right)\right]

\displaystyle={\rm tr}_{{}_{\!\!YM}}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\hat{F}^{ab}\hat{F}_{ab}-\frac{8}{3}\hat{F}_{ab}B^{a}B^{b}+\frac{8}{3}B_{a}B_{b}B^{a}B^{b}-\frac{32}{3}B^{4}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{4}{3}D_{a}B_{b}D^{a}B^{b}-\frac{2}{3}DBDB\bigg]\,.

(B.54)

The analogous results for
a_{2}(R_{\psi_{c}})
are obtained by replacing
A\to-A^{T}
and
B\to B^{T}

(and also
T^{\alpha}\to T^{\alpha T}
for the explicit
T^{\alpha}
appearing in the traces).
Their effect is just to double the contribution from
a_{2}(R_{\psi})
in the chiral and trace anomalies.

B.5
Dirac mass in MAT background

We give here a brief description of a different regularization that could be employed for a Dirac fermion coupled to the MAT backgound discussed in sec.
4.2
.
There, we used a Majorana mass for the PV fields, arguing that it was most convenient as it breaks the least number of symmetries.
We give support here for that claim, by showing how a Dirac mass for the PV fields could be coupled to the MAT background.

In a flat background the Dirac mass is given by

-M\bar{\psi}\psi=\frac{1}{2}M\left(\psi_{c}^{T}C\psi+\psi^{T}C\psi_{c}\right)\;.

(B.55)

This term
breaks the
U(1)_{A}
axial symmetry while maintaining the
U(1)_{V}
vector symmetry.
This continues to be the case also when one tries to MAT-covariantize it.
There are various options to couple the Dirac mass to the MAT geometry.
We may choose to use only the metric
g_{\mu\nu}
in the mass term, without any axial extension,
so that

\Delta_{\scriptscriptstyle D}\mathcal{L}=-\sqrt{g}M\bar{\psi}\psi=\frac{\sqrt{g}}{2}M(\psi^{T}_{c}C\psi+\psi^{T}C\psi_{c})

(B.56)

has the virtue of preserving the vector-like diffeomorphisms and vector-like local Lorentz transformations
on top of the
U(1)_{V}
symmetry, while breaking all of their axial extensions.
It also breaks both vector and axial Weyl symmetries,
which are then expected to be anomalous as well.
Counterterms should eventually be introduced to achieve the equivalence
with the results in sec.
4.2.4
.
Other choices are also possible, e.g.
\sqrt{g}\to\tfrac{1}{2}(\sqrt{g_{+}}+\sqrt{g_{-}})
,
which shares the same property of
preserving the vector-like diffeomorphisms and local Lorentz transformations.

In the following we limit ourselves to derive the regulators to be used for computing
the anomalies in this scheme.
We add to the lagrangian (
4.37
) written in a symmetric form

\mathcal{L}=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{c}^{T}C\sqrt{\bar{\hat{g}}}\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}\psi+\frac{1}{2}\psi^{T}C\sqrt{\hat{g}}\bar{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\psi_{c}

(B.57)

the mass term (
B.56
), and comparing it with (
2.79
) we find,
choosing the field basis

\phi=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi\\
\psi_{c}\end{array}\right)
,

T\mathcal{O}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&C\sqrt{\hat{g}}\bar{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\\
C\sqrt{\bar{\hat{g}}}{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\quad T=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\sqrt{g}C\\
\sqrt{g}C&0\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.58)

and thus

\mathcal{O}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\hat{g}}}{g}}\,{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}&0\\
0&\sqrt{\frac{\hat{g}}{g}}\,\bar{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\quad\mathcal{O}^{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\hat{g}}}{g}}\,{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\hat{g}}}{g}}\,{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}&0\\
0&\sqrt{\frac{\hat{g}}{g}}\,\bar{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\sqrt{\frac{\hat{g}}{g}}\,\bar{\hat{\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,}}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.59)

with the differential operators acting on everything placed on their right hand side.
This regulator
\mathcal{R}=-\mathcal{O}^{2}
is difficult to work with, but it has the virtue of being
covariant under vector diffeomorphisms.
Its structure is again more transparent when splitting the Dirac fermion into its chiral parts, so that on the basis

\phi=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\lambda\\
\rho\\
\rho_{c}\\
\lambda_{c}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.60)

we find a block diagonal regulator

\mathcal{R}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}R_{\lambda}&0&0&0\\
0&R_{\rho}&0&0\\
0&0&R_{\rho_{c}}&0\\
0&0&0&R_{\lambda_{c}}\end{array}\right)\,,

(B.61)

with entries

\displaystyle R_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{-}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{-}\sqrt{\frac{g_{+}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{+}P_{+}\;,\qquad R_{\rho}=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{+}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{+}\sqrt{\frac{g_{-}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{-}P_{-}

(B.62)

\displaystyle R_{\lambda_{c}}

\displaystyle=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{-}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{-}\sqrt{\frac{g_{+}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{+}P_{-}\;,\qquad R_{\rho_{c}}=-\sqrt{\frac{g_{+}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{+}\sqrt{\frac{g_{-}}{g}}\nabla\!\!\!\!/\,_{-}P_{+}\;.

The functions
\sqrt{\frac{g_{\mp}}{g}}
are scalar functions under
the vector-like diffeomorphism, so that these regulators are covariant under that symmetry.
The projectors
P_{\pm}
take just the unit value on the corresponding chiral spinor space,
but we have kept them to remind on which space the different regulators act.
A systematic analysis of all the anomalies, including the axial gravitational anomaly,
may be feasible in this scheme, at least when treating

f_{\mu\nu}
as a perturbation.
The final results have to agree with the anomalies presented in sec.
4.2.4
by adding suitable counterterms to the effective action.

Anhang
C
Further details on the holographic anomalies with scalar fields

C.1
Ansätze for the backreaction

In this appendix we collect the Ansätze that we make to solve eqs. (
5.21
), (
5.22
) for
n=1,2
.
The explicit results can be found in the Mathematica notebook provided in the supplementary material of
[
Bro22aa
]
.
1
1

1

The results were obtained with the help of the xAct collection of Mathematica packages
[
MPM07aa
,
MYP08aa
,
Mar08aa
,
BMM09aa
,
Nut14aa
]
.

In the following, we assume that
g_{ij}\equiv g^{(0)}_{ij}
and curvatures and covariant derivatives are w.r.t.
g^{(0)}_{ij}
, and
\phi\equiv\phi_{(0)}
.
We also choose
h_{0}=0
, as explained in the text.

The solution of the PBH equation for
g^{(1)}_{ij}
is given in (
2.143
) and it is not modified by the presence of the backreaction.
The most generic Ansatz for
h^{(2)}_{ij}
has thirty-five terms and six coefficients will not be fixed by the modified PBH equation.
We write the Ansatz as

\displaystyle h^{(2)}_{ij}=

\displaystyle\;+h_{21}g_{ij}R_{kl}R^{kl}\phi^{2}+h_{22}R_{ij}R\phi^{2}+h_{23}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}+h_{24}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R+h_{25}R_{ij}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+h_{26}g_{ij}R\phi\Box\phi+\beta_{1}R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+\beta_{2}g_{ij}R^{2}\phi^{2}+\beta_{3}R_{i}{}^{klm}R_{jklm}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{4}g_{ij}R_{klmn}R^{klmn}\phi^{2}+\beta_{6}R\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla_{j}\phi+\beta_{5}(\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla_{j}R+\phi\nabla_{i}R\nabla_{j}\phi)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{7}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\beta_{8}(R_{j}{}^{k}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{i}\phi+R_{i}{}^{k}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi)+\beta_{9}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}+\beta_{10}g_{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{11}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi+\beta_{12}(\nabla_{j}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi)+\beta_{13}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{14}g_{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\phi\nabla^{k}R+\beta_{15}(\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{k}\phi+\phi\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}\phi)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{16}(R_{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\phi+R_{ik}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\phi)+\beta_{17}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\phi+\beta_{18}R_{ij}\nabla_{k}\phi\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{19}g_{ij}R\nabla_{k}\phi\nabla^{k}\phi+\beta_{20}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\phi+\beta_{21}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{22}g_{ij}R^{kl}\phi\nabla_{l}\nabla_{k}\phi+\beta_{23}g_{ij}\Box\phi\Box\phi+\beta_{24}g_{ij}\nabla^{k}\phi\Box\nabla_{k}\phi+\beta_{25}g_{ij}\phi\Box^{2}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{26}g_{ij}R_{kl}\nabla^{k}\phi\nabla^{l}\phi+\beta_{27}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{k}\phi\nabla^{l}\phi+\beta_{28}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\beta_{29}g_{ij}\nabla_{l}\nabla_{k}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi\,,

(C.1)

and leave the coefficients
h_{21},\ldots,h_{26}
free.
The full solution is in the aforementioned Mathematica notebook.

A similar Ansatz is made for
\tilde{h}^{(2)}_{ij}
and its coefficients are fixed in terms of
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
by the modified PBH equation.

The solution for
g^{(2)}_{ij}
is given in (
2.144
) and it is not modified by the presence of the backreaction.
Thus, here we focus on
{h}^{(3)}_{ij}
, which appears in eq. (
5.36
) only as
{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}
and we then do not compute
{h}^{(3)}_{ij}
but rather its trace that satisfies the following PBH equation

\delta g^{(2)i}{}_{i}+\delta{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}=-2\sigma g^{(2)i}{}_{i}+g^{(0)ij}\delta g^{(2)}{}_{ij}+2\sigma\beta^{ij}g^{(2)}_{ij}-2\sigma{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}+g^{(0)ij}\delta{h}^{(3)}_{ij}\,.

(C.2)

The most generic Ansatz for
{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}
has sixty-six terms and nine coefficients will not be fixed by the modified PBH equation.
We write the Ansatz as

\displaystyle{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}=

\displaystyle\;h_{31}R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi+h_{32}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R+h_{33}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}+h_{34}R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+h_{35}R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi+h_{36}R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}+h_{37}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}+h_{38}R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+h_{39}R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi+\gamma_{1}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+\gamma_{2}R^{3}\phi^{2}+\gamma_{3}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{4}R^{ij}R_{i}{}^{klm}R_{jklm}\phi^{2}+\gamma_{5}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}+\gamma_{6}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{7}R\phi^{2}\Box R+\gamma_{8}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi+\gamma_{9}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi+\gamma_{10}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{11}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+\gamma_{12}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{13}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{14}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{15}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{16}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R+\gamma_{17}\phi\Box R\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{18}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\gamma_{19}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\gamma_{20}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}R+\gamma_{21}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{22}R\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+\gamma_{23}\phi^{2}\Box^{2}R+\gamma_{24}R\phi\Box^{2}\phi+\gamma_{25}R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{26}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\gamma_{27}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\gamma_{28}R_{i}{}^{klm}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{29}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi+\gamma_{30}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\gamma_{31}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{32}R_{i}{}^{klm}R_{jklm}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{33}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{34}R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{35}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi+\gamma_{36}R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\gamma_{37}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi\Box R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{38}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}+\gamma_{39}R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi+\gamma_{40}\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{41}R^{ij}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\gamma_{42}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\gamma_{43}\Box\phi\Box^{2}\phi+\gamma_{44}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{45}\phi\Box^{3}\phi+\gamma_{46}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\gamma_{47}\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{48}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\gamma_{49}R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi+\gamma_{50}\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{51}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi+\gamma_{52}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi+\gamma_{53}R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{54}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\gamma_{55}R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi+\gamma_{56}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\gamma_{57}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\,,

(C.3)

and leave the coefficients
h_{31},\ldots,h_{39}
free.
The full solution is in the aforementioned Mathematica notebook.

Similarly, since
\tilde{h}^{(3)}
appears in eq. (
5.36
) only as
\tilde{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}
, we do not compute
\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{ij}
but rather its trace that satisfies the following PBH equation

\delta\tilde{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}=-2\sigma\tilde{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}+g^{(0)ij}\delta\tilde{h}^{(3)}_{ij}\,.

(C.4)

A similar Ansatz to (
C.3
) is made for
\tilde{h}^{(3)i}{}_{i}
and its coefficients are fixed in terms of
h_{1},h_{2},c_{1},c_{2}
by the modified PBH equation.

C.2
Gravitational action with backreaction

From (
2.151
) in FG coordinates with backreaction (
5.6
) we identify:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{h}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}d(1+4\gamma)h^{i}{}_{i}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}z(1+4\gamma)\big[2(d-1)g^{\prime}_{ij}h^{ij}-(d-1)g^{ij}g^{\prime}_{ij}h^{k}{}_{k}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2(d-1)g^{ij}h^{\prime}_{ij}+h^{ij}R_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}h^{i}{}_{i}R-\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}h_{ij}+\Box h^{i}{}_{i}\big]

\displaystyle+z^{2}\big[-2(1+4\gamma)g^{\prime\prime}_{ij}h^{ij}+(3-4(d-5)\gamma)g^{im}g^{jn}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}h^{jk}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-(1+8\gamma)g^{ij}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{kl}+(1+4\gamma)g^{ij}g^{\prime\prime}_{ij}h^{k}{}_{k}-2\gamma R_{ikjl}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}h^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-(\tfrac{3}{4}-(d-5)\gamma)g^{im}g^{jn}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{mn}h^{k}{}_{k}+(\tfrac{1}{4}+2\gamma)(g^{ij}g^{\prime}_{ij})^{2}h^{k}{}_{k}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-(3-4(d-5)\gamma)g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{\prime}_{ij}h^{\prime}_{kl}+2(1+4\gamma)g^{ij}h^{\prime\prime}_{ij}+2\gamma g^{\prime}_{ij}R^{ij}h^{k}{}_{k}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+(1+8\gamma)g^{ij}g^{kl}g^{\prime}{ij}h^{\prime}_{kl}+4\gamma h^{\prime}_{ij}R^{ij}-8\gamma g^{\prime}_{ij}h^{i}{}_{k}R^{jk}-\gamma h^{ij}R_{i}{}^{klp}R_{jklp}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{4}\gamma h^{i}{}_{i}R_{jklp}R^{jklp}-2\gamma g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}h^{k}{}_{k}+4\gamma g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla^{j}h^{ik}-2\gamma g^{\prime}_{ij}\Box h^{ij}\big]

\displaystyle+\gamma z^{3}\big[-12g^{im}g^{jn}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{jl}h^{kl}+8g^{ij}g^{mn}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{lm}g^{\prime}_{mk}h^{kl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+4g^{im}g^{jn}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{kl}+2g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kl}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{jl}h^{p}{}_{p}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2g^{ij}g^{km}g^{nl}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}g^{\prime}_{mn}h^{p}{}_{p}+12g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kl}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}h^{\prime}_{jl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-8g^{ij}g^{km}g^{ln}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{\prime}_{mn}-4g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{mn}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{\prime}_{mn}-g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{p}{}_{p}R^{ikjl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-4g^{\prime}_{ij}h^{\prime}_{kl}R^{ikjl}-6g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{i}{}_{p}R^{jklp}-4g^{km}g^{ln}h^{ij}\nabla_{i}g^{\prime}_{kl}\nabla_{j}g^{\prime}_{mn}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-4g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla^{i}g^{\prime}_{kl}\nabla^{j}h^{kl}-8g^{il}\nabla^{j}h^{\prime}_{ik}\nabla^{k}g^{\prime}_{jl}+8g^{ik}g^{jl}\nabla_{k}h^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla^{k}g^{\prime}_{kl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+8g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla^{i}g^{\prime}_{kl}\nabla^{l}h^{jk}-2g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{j}h^{ik}+2g^{\prime ij}g^{\prime kl}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}h^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+8g^{kn}h^{ij}\nabla_{j}g^{\prime}_{kl}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{in}+4g^{in}g^{\prime}_{jn}\nabla^{j}h^{kl}\nabla_{l}g^{\prime}_{ik}+4h^{ij}\nabla^{k}g^{\prime}_{jl}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{ik}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-4g^{in}g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla^{k}h^{jl}\nabla_{l}g^{\prime}_{kn}-8g^{kn}h^{ij}\nabla_{l}g^{\prime}_{jk}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{in}-4g^{in}g^{\prime}_{ij}\nabla_{l}h^{jk}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{kn}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2g^{jn}h^{i}{}_{i}\nabla^{k}g^{\prime}_{jl}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{kn}+2g^{jm}g^{km}h^{i}{}_{i}\nabla_{l}g^{\prime}_{jk}\nabla^{l}g^{\prime}_{mn}\big]

\displaystyle+\gamma z^{4}\big[8g^{im}g^{kn}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}g^{\prime\prime}_{mn}h^{jl}-16g^{il}g^{\prime\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime\prime}_{jl}h^{jk}+16g^{im}g^{jn}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime\prime}_{jl}h^{kl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+4g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{\prime\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime\prime}_{kl}h^{k}{}_{k}-4g^{kq}g^{ir}g^{js}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{rs}g^{\prime}_{jl}g^{\prime}_{pq}h^{lp}-8g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kl}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}h^{\prime\prime}_{jl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-4g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{mr}g^{ns}g^{pq}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{rq}h^{ps}-4g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kl}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime\prime}_{jl}h^{p}{}_{p}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kr}g^{ls}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{jl}g^{\prime}_{rs}h^{p}{}_{p}+\tfrac{1}{2}(g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{kl})^{2}h^{p}{}_{p}-16g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kl}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime\prime}_{ik}h^{\prime}_{jl}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+4g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kr}g^{ls}g^{\prime}_{ik}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{jl}h^{\prime}_{rs}+4g^{im}g^{jn}g^{kr}g^{ls}g^{\prime}_{ij}g^{\prime}_{mn}g^{\prime}_{kl}h^{\prime}_{rs}+16g^{ik}g^{jl}g^{\prime\prime}_{ij}h^{\prime\prime}_{kl}\big]\,,

(C.5)

while
\mathcal{L}_{g}
is still given by (
2.152
).
Notice that also terms of
\mathcal{O}(z^{4})
contribute to the anomaly in
d=4
, since
h^{\prime\prime}_{ij}=-z^{-2}\tilde{h}^{(1)}_{ij}+\mathcal{O}(z^{-1})
.

C.3
Coefficients renormalisation

Evaluating (
2.154
) and (
5.36
) on the PBH solutions, we find that
\mathcal{L}_{g}^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}_{h}^{(2)}
satisfies the WZ condition (
5.34
), even though it is singular in
d=4
.
In particular, expanding around
d=4
we find that

\mathcal{L}_{g}^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}_{h}^{(2)}=\frac{1}{d-4}I^{(-1)}+I^{(0)}+(d-4)I^{(1)}+(d-4)^{2}I^{(2)}+\ldots\,,

(C.6)

with (
g_{ij}\equiv g^{(0)}_{ij}
and
\phi\equiv\phi_{(0)}
)

\displaystyle I^{(-1)}

\displaystyle=c\,h_{1}\Bigg(\frac{7}{24}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\frac{1}{24}R^{3}\phi^{2}-\frac{1}{2}R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}+\frac{1}{24}R\phi^{2}\Box R-\frac{1}{12}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{1}{12}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R+\frac{1}{3}R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\frac{1}{12}\phi\Box R\Box\phi+\frac{1}{6}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{1}{4}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}-\frac{1}{2}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}+\frac{1}{4}R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi-R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi\Bigg)\,.

(C.7)

Notice that
I^{(-1)}
is entirely type B and
\delta I^{(-1)}=-4\sigma I^{(-1)}
under Weyl transformation up to
\mathcal{O}(\phi^{2})
.
However,
\mathcal{L}_{g}^{(2)}+\mathcal{L}_{h}^{(2)}
contains also several free coefficients, which are not fixed by the PBH equations, i.e.
\{c_{1},c_{2},h_{1},h_{21},\ldots,h_{26},h_{31},\ldots,h_{39}\}
, and it is possible to regularise (
C.6
) by renormalising some of these coefficients.
In particular, there is a unique shift which cancels
I^{(-1)}
and it consists of shifting some of the free coefficients which appear in
I^{(2)}
as follows:

\displaystyle h_{32}\to h_{32}+\frac{c\,h_{1}}{18a(d-4)^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle h_{33}\to h_{33}+\frac{7c\,h_{1}}{36a(d-4)^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle h_{34}\to h_{34}+\frac{2c\,h_{1}}{9a(d-4)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle h_{36}\to h_{36}-\frac{c\,h_{1}}{3a(d-4)^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle h_{37}\to h_{37}-\frac{c\,h_{1}}{6a(d-4)^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle h_{38}\to h_{38}+\frac{c\,h_{1}}{6a(d-4)^{2}}\,.

Since the shifted coefficients only appear in
I^{(2)}
, this shift does not introduce new singularities, and of course the regularised expression satisfies the Wess-Zumino condition.

C.4
Complete expression of the anomaly

We provide here the complete expression of the holographic anomaly
\mathcal{A}^{4d}
as in (
5.37
), recalling that in the type A and B pure gravity anomalies with the respective scalar field contributions we identify the following terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{B}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}^{\text{pg}}_{\text{B}}+\mathcal{I}_{1}(h_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{2}(c_{1},h_{1},h_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{3}(c_{2},h_{1},h_{2})\,,

(C.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{A}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}^{\text{pg}}_{\text{A}}+\mathcal{I}_{4}(h_{1})+\mathcal{I}_{5}(h_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{6}(h_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{7}(c_{1},h_{1},h_{2})+\mathcal{I}_{8}(c_{2},h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\mathcal{I}_{9}(h_{21})+\mathcal{I}_{10}(h_{22})+\mathcal{I}_{11}(h_{23})+\mathcal{I}_{12}(h_{24})+\mathcal{I}_{13}(h_{25})+\mathcal{I}_{14}(h_{26})\,.

(C.9)

We proceed by presenting and analysing them individually.
Again, we assume that
g_{ij}\equiv g^{(0)}_{ij}
and curvatures and covariant derivatives are w.r.t.
g^{(0)}_{ij}
, and
\phi\equiv\phi_{(0)}
.
The interested reader can also find the expressions of
\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{B}}
and
\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{A}}
in the Mathematica notebook provided in the supplementary material of
[
Bro22aa
]
.

As for
\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{B}}
, we already presented
\mathcal{I}^{\text{pg}}_{\text{B}}
and
\mathcal{I}_{1}
in (
5.42
).
We move then to
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, which is given by

\mathcal{I}_{2}(c_{1},h_{1},h_{2})=4c\,c_{1}(h_{1}+4h_{2})\hat{R}\,C^{2}\,.

(C.10)

This tensorial structure leads to a Weyl invariant anomaly integrand, as already pointed out in
[
ST19aa
]
.
Thus, in the CFT it contributes to the anomaly as an ‘ordinary’ anomaly, which satisfy the WZ condition without need of the beta-function.
Henceforth we will refer to this kind of structures as ‘ordinary anomaly’ for short.
Notice that
\mathcal{I}_{2}
vanishes in flat space.
A similar discussion follows for
\mathcal{I}_{3}
since it is related to
\mathcal{I}_{2}
by

\mathcal{I}_{3}(c_{2},h_{1},h_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{2}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{4},h_{1},h_{2}\right)\,.

(C.11)

As for
\mathcal{A}^{4d}_{\text{A}}
, the tensorial structures
\mathcal{I}^{\text{pg}}_{\text{A}}
and
\mathcal{I}_{4}
are given in (
5.43
) and
\mathcal{I}_{5}
is also already presented and discussed in the main text.
Then, we move to
\mathcal{I}_{6}
which corresponds to ordinary anomalies and thus satisfies the WZ condition without need of the metric beta-function.
Explicitly, it reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{6}(h_{2})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{2}\Big(3\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-\tfrac{15}{2}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{15}{8}\Box\phi\Box^{2}\phi+\tfrac{9}{8}\phi\Box^{2}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{15}{2}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{3}{8}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{5}{2}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\tfrac{23}{64}R^{3}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{39}{8}R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{17}{64}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{33}{4}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{9}{32}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{16}R\phi^{2}\Box R-\tfrac{17}{8}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{15}{8}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{15}{4}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+\tfrac{99}{4}R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{129}{8}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{189}{8}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{23}{8}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{51}{4}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{19}{8}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R+6R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{17}{8}\phi\Box R\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{17}{8}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{1}{8}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}R+\tfrac{21}{8}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{15}{4}R\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{16}\phi^{2}\Box^{2}R+\tfrac{3}{2}R\phi\Box^{2}\phi-\tfrac{27}{4}R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{27}{2}R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{37}{4}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{15}{2}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{7}{4}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{23}{4}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{59}{8}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{57}{4}R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}+\tfrac{15}{4}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi+\tfrac{27}{2}R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{9}{4}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}-\tfrac{39}{4}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}+\tfrac{21}{2}R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{15}{4}R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-9R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi+\tfrac{129}{16}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-\tfrac{9}{32}\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\tfrac{45}{4}R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi+3\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{21}{2}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-18R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}-\tfrac{75}{4}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{99}{4}R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi-\tfrac{3}{4}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi-\tfrac{237}{64}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,.

(C.12)

Notice that
\mathcal{I}_{6}
vanishes in flat space after integration by parts.

The tensorial structures in
\mathcal{I}_{7}
correspond to Weyl invariant ordinary anomalies and they are thus solutions of the WZ condition without need of the metric beta-function.
Explicitly, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{7}(c_{1},h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle=a\,c_{1}\Big(-48h_{1}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+4(9h_{1}+8h_{2})R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{16}{3}(h_{1}+h_{2})R^{3}\phi^{2}-4(h_{1}+4h_{2})RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}-4h_{1}R\phi^{2}\Box R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-4(5h_{1}+8h_{2})R^{2}\phi\Box\phi-12(h_{1}+8h_{2})R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-8h_{1}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+48h_{1}R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi+24h_{1}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-48(3h_{1}+8h_{2})R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+8(3h_{1}+8h_{2})R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+24(3h_{1}+8h_{2})R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+64h_{1}R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-16h_{1}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+48h_{1}R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi+16h_{1}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-96h_{1}R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}-96h_{1}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi+24h_{1}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+24(3h_{1}+8h_{2})R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi+96h_{1}R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-96h_{1}R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi+192h_{1}R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-48h_{1}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-96h_{1}R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-96h_{1}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi\Big)\,.

(C.13)

Notice that
\mathcal{I}_{7}
vanishes in flat space.
Similar properties hold for
\mathcal{I}_{8}
since it is related to
\mathcal{I}_{7}
by

\mathcal{I}_{8}(c_{2},h_{1},h_{2})=\mathcal{I}_{7}\left(\frac{c_{2}}{4},h_{1},h_{2}\right)\,.

(C.14)

The remaining tensorial structures
\mathcal{I}_{9},\ldots,\mathcal{I}_{14}
are parametrised by the free coefficients which appear in
h^{(2)}_{ij}
.
Each of them solves the WZ condition without need of the metric beta-function and in the field theory they can be eliminated with counterterms.
Thus, they correspond to trivial solutions of the WZ condition.
Explicitly, they read:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{9}(h_{21})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{21}\Big(-6R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+6R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{4}R^{3}\phi^{2}-6R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{4}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}+6R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}R\phi^{2}\Box R-\tfrac{1}{2}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R-2R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+12R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi-6R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{1}{2}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+3R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi+3\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-6R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,,

(C.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{10}(h_{22})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{22}\Big(3\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-3\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi+3R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}+\tfrac{3}{16}R^{3}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{3}{16}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{8}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}R\phi^{2}\Box R+\tfrac{3}{4}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{5}{8}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R+\tfrac{3}{2}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R-9R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{9}{4}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{8}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{8}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\phi\Box R\Box\phi+R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi+3R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+3R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+2\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R-R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}+6R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi-3R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}-6\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}-3R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi-6R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+6R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi+3\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-\tfrac{9}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+3R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi-3\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-3R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}+9R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{8}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,,

(C.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{11}(h_{23})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{23}\Big(\tfrac{3}{2}\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{9}{4}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\tfrac{9}{32}R^{3}\phi^{2}-3R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{9}{32}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{9}{4}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{9}{16}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{1}{8}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{8}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{1}{16}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{1}{4}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+\tfrac{3}{2}R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{15}{8}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{1}{16}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{9}{16}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R-R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}\phi\Box R\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{1}{4}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi-\tfrac{1}{2}R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{2}R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{4}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{4}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{1}{2}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{2}R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}+3R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}-3R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi+3R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{4}\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\tfrac{3}{2}R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\tfrac{9}{2}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi-\tfrac{9}{16}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,,

(C.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{12}(h_{24})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{24}\Big(3\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-3\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi-6R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{15}{2}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\tfrac{15}{16}R^{3}\phi^{2}-9R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{15}{16}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{15}{2}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{15}{8}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{1}{4}R\phi^{2}\Box R+\tfrac{1}{2}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{1}{8}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{3}{2}R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R+9R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{27}{4}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{8}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{15}{8}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R-4R^{ij}R\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}\phi\Box R\Box\phi+R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi-R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{7}{2}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+9R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi+3R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}+6R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}+3\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi\Box R_{ij}-3\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-6R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi+6R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi+3\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+3R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi-3\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-3R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}-9R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+6R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi+9R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi-\tfrac{15}{8}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,,

(C.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{13}(h_{25})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{25}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-4\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\Box\phi\Box^{2}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{7}{2}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{5}{2}R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{9}{4}R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\tfrac{9}{32}R^{3}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{9}{32}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{9}{4}R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{9}{16}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{4}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{1}{16}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2R^{jk}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{21}{8}R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}R_{jk}R^{jk}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{16}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{13}{16}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi-\tfrac{1}{4}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}R+\tfrac{1}{4}\phi\Box R\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{4}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+R\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}R\phi\Box^{2}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{4}R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+\tfrac{7}{2}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{4}\nabla^{i}R\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{1}{2}\phi\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}R-\tfrac{1}{2}R^{jk}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi-\tfrac{7}{2}R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{2}R^{ij}\phi^{2}\Box R_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{2}\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}R_{ij}R^{ij}\phi\Box\phi-2R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi-2R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-2R^{ij}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\phi^{2}\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+\tfrac{5}{4}\phi^{2}\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-\tfrac{7}{2}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{2}R^{ij}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\phi-\tfrac{5}{2}\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}R_{ij}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{2}R_{ijkl}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}R^{jk}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}+R^{ij}R_{ikjl}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{1}{2}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}\phi-\tfrac{9}{16}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,,

(C.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{14}(h_{26})

\displaystyle=a\,h_{26}\Big(3\nabla^{j}\Box\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-12\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi+3\Box\phi\Box^{2}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-12\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi-6R_{i}{}^{k}R^{ij}R_{jk}\phi^{2}+6R_{ij}R^{ij}R\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{4}R^{3}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-6R^{ij}R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}-\tfrac{3}{4}RR_{ijkl}R^{ijkl}\phi^{2}+6R_{i}{}^{m}{}_{k}{}^{n}R^{ijkl}R_{jmln}\phi^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{3}{2}R_{ij}{}^{mn}R^{ijkl}R_{klmn}\phi^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}R\phi^{2}\Box R+\tfrac{1}{4}R^{2}\phi\Box\phi-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\tfrac{1}{4}\phi^{2}\nabla_{i}R\nabla^{i}R+R\phi\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R-6R^{jklm}\phi\nabla_{i}R_{jklm}\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{1}{4}R^{2}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\tfrac{3}{2}R_{jklm}R^{jklm}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}\phi\Box R\Box\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}R\Box\phi\Box\phi+3\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3\phi\nabla^{i}R\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+3R\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{i}\phi+\tfrac{3}{2}R\phi\Box^{2}\phi-3R_{ij}\phi\nabla^{i}R\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+3R_{i}{}^{k}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi-3R_{ij}R\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi+12R^{kl}R_{ikjl}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{j}\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-12R^{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi-6\phi\nabla^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box R_{ij}-6R^{ij}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\Box\phi

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-3R_{i}{}^{j}\nabla^{i}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\phi-6R^{ij}\phi\Box\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi-12\phi\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{i}\phi\nabla^{k}R^{ij}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-12\nabla_{i}R_{jk}\nabla^{i}\phi\nabla^{k}\nabla^{j}\phi-6R_{ikjl}\phi^{2}\nabla^{l}\nabla^{k}R^{ij}-\tfrac{3}{2}\phi^{2}\nabla_{m}R_{ijkl}\nabla^{m}R^{ijkl}\Big)\,.

(C.20)

Notice that
\mathcal{I}_{9}
vanishes in flat space and similarly
\mathcal{I}_{10},\ldots,\mathcal{I}_{14}
after integration by parts.
